NOTICE 
Notice i
s hereby given by Borough Council for the Borough of Wyomissing, Berks County, 
Pennsylvania, that Council will hold a public hearing on November 10, 2026, at 7:00 PM at 
Borough Hall, 22 Reading Boulevard, Wyomissing, Pennsylvania  to consider and receive public 
comment on two proposed ordinances to amend Chapter 27 (Zoning) and an ordinance to amend 
Chapter 22 (Subdivision and Land Development Ordinance) of the Code of Ordinances of the 
Borough of Wyomissing as  summarized below. Following the conclusion of the hearing, the 
proposed ordinances will be considered for adoption at the regularly scheduled public meeting of 
the Council of the Borough of Wyomissing, which will immediately follow the aforementioned 
public hearing on November 10, 2026.   
The titl
e of the first proposed ordinance to amend the Zoning Ordinance is “ AN ORDINANCE 
OF THE BOROUGH OF WYOMISSING, BERKS COUNTY, PENNSYLVANIA, 
AMENDING CHAPTER 27 (ZONING) OF THE CODE OF ORDINANCES OF THE 
BOROUGH OF WYOMISSING BY REPEALING AND RESTATING PART 5 -A, 
COMMERCIAL AND INDUSTRIAL REDEVELOPMENT OVERLAY DISTRICT, IN 
ITS ENTIRETY; EXPANDING THE BOUNDARIES OF THE OVERLAY DISTRICT; 
ESTABLISHING PERMITTED USES, ESTABLISHING DEVELOPMENT 
STANDARDS; AND PROVIDING FOR SEVERABILITY, REPEAL OF INCONSISTENT 
ORDINANCES,  AN EFFECTIVE DATE, AND MODIFICATION OF CODE OF 
ORDINANCES.” 
If enact
ed, the proposed ordinance will repeal and restate Chapter 27, Part 5 -A of the Code of 
Ordinances, to update the Commercial and Industrial Redevelopment Overlay District, and provide 
criteria for re development of qualified property within the district. The proposed ordinance 
includes provisions for fee-simple subdivision of individual buildings and building pads, shared 
parking and shared access among multiple lots under separate ownership, shared stormwater 
management and utility infrastructure administered by a mandatory lot owners a ssociation, 
incentives for bicycle and pedestrian improvements, and enhanced greenspace, shade tree, and 
landscaping standards within large parking areas and streetscape improvement standards along 
public streets adjacent to redevelopment sites.  If enacted, the proposed Ordinance will also revise 
the boundaries of the Commercial and Industrial Redevelopment Overlay District.   
The titl
e of the second proposed ordinance to amend the Zoning Ordinance is “AN ORDINANCE 
OF THE BOROUGH OF WYOMISSING, BERKS COUNTY, PENNSYLVANIA, 
AMENDING THE BOROUGH OF WYOMISSING CODE OF ORDINANCES, CHAPTER 
27 ENTITLED “ZONING” TO ADD THE DEFINITION OF “LANDSCAPE 
ARCHITECT”, TO IMPOSE UNIFORM, DETAILED REQUIREMENT FOR 
LANDSCAPE PLANS AS PART OF PRELIMINARY AND FINAL LAND 
DEVELOPMENT PLANS, AND REVISING THE EXISTING LANGUAGE FOR 
UNIFORMITY.” 
DRAFT

If enacted, the proposed ordinance will amend the Zoning Ordinance to require detailed landscape 
plans, as part of the land development process in the Borough. The proposed ordinance also sets 
forth the regulations for said landscaping plans  including but not limited to the protection of 
existing trees, required street trees, required screen buffers and site element screens, softening 
buffers, and parking lot landscaping. The proposed ordinance also sets forth lists of the 
recommended shade, ornamental, and evergreen trees for such landscape plans within the 
Borough.     
The ti
tle of the proposed ordinance to amend the Subdivision and Land Development Ordinance 
is “AN ORDINANCE OF THE BOROUGH OF WYOMISSING, BERKS COUNTY, 
PENNSYLV ANIA, AMENDING THE BOROUGH OF WYOMISSING CODE OF 
ORDINANCES, CHAPTER 22 ENTITLED "SUBDIVISION AND LAND 
DEVELOPMENT", PART 2 ENTITLED "DEFINITIONS", SECTION 204 ENTITLED 
"SPECIFIC TERMS",  TO REVISE THE DEFINITION OF LANDSCAPE ARCHITECT, 
AND TO AMEND PART 4 ENTITLED “PLAN REQUIREMENTS”, SECTION 403 
ENTITLED “PRELIMINARY PLAN”,  PARAGRAPH (1) TO REQUIRE SUBMISSION 
OF A LANDSCAPE PLAN WITH A PRELIMINARY PLAN, AND TO AMEND PART 4 
ENTITLED “PLAN REQUIREMENTS”, SECTION 404 “FINAL PLAN” TO REQUIRE 
SUBMISSION OF A LANDSCAPE PLAN WITH A FINAL PLAN.” 
If enact
ed, the proposed ordinance will amend, the Subdivision and Land Development Ordinance, 
to require landscape plans as part of both preliminary and final land development plans, and to 
clarify the terms used.  The proposed ordinance will require the landscape plan to be submitted per 
the requirements of Chapter 27, Zoning Ordinance, including but not limited to the protection of 
existing trees, required street trees, required screen buffers and site element screens, softening 
buffers, and parking lot landscaping.  
The ful
l text of the proposed ordinance s may be examined without charge at the Borough office, 
22 Reading Boulevard, Wyomissing, PA, during regular business hours which are Monday through 
Friday, 9:00 a.m. to 3:00 p.m. Copies of the proposed ordinances have  also been filed with the 
newspaper publishing this notice and at the Berks County Law Library, 633 Court Street, Reading, 
PA 19601. Borough Hall is handicapped accessible. However, anyone requiring special 
accommodations to facilitate participation at the meeting may call the Borough Manager at ( 610) 
376-7481 to make such arrangements.

DRAFT

BOROUGH OF WYOMISSING 
BERKS COUNTY, PENNSYLVANIA 
ORDINANCE NO. 1464-2026 
AN ORDINANCE OF THE BOROUGH OF WYOMISSING, BERKS COUNTY, 
PENNSYLVANIA, AMENDING CHAPTER 27 (ZONING) OF THE CODE OF 
ORDINANCES OF THE BOROUGH OF WYOMISSING BY REPEALING AND 
RESTATING PART 5 -A, COMMERCIAL AND INDUSTRIAL REDEVELOPMENT 
OVERLAY DISTRICT, IN ITS  ENTIRETY; EXPANDING THE BOUNDARIES OF 
THE OVERLAY DISTRICT; ESTABLISHING PERMITTED USES, ESTABLISHING 
DEVELOPMENT STANDARDS; AND PROVIDING FOR SEVERABILITY, REPEAL 
OF INCONSISTENT ORDINANCES,  AN EFFECTIVE DATE, AND MODIFICATION 
OF CODE OF ORDINANCES. 
WHEREAS, the Borough of Wyomissing (the “Borough”) previously established the 
Commercial and Industrial Redevelopment Overlay District by Ordinance No. 1286, as amended by 
Ordinance No. 1381 and Ordinance No. 1397, codified at Chapter 27, Part 5- A, of the Borough Code 
of Ordinances; and 
WHEREAS, Borough Council finds that the comprehensive redevelopment of aging regional 
commercial sites requires flexible tools not fully provided by the existing overlay district, including 
provisions for fee -simple subdivision of individual buildings and building pads, shared parking and 
shared access among multiple lots under separate ownership, shared stormwater management and 
utility infrastructure administered by a mandatory lot owners association, incentives for bicycle and 
pedestrian improvements, and enhanced greenspace, shade tree, and landscaping standards within large 
parking areas and streetscape improvement standards along public streets adjacent to redevelopment 
sites, in each case coordinated with the Official Bike Route Map of the Borough of Wyomissing and 
the Borough of West Reading and with the landscaping regulations of §604 of the Zoning Ordinance; 
and 
WHEREAS, Section 605 of the Pennsylvania Municipalities Planning Code, 53 P.S. §10605, 
authorizes the creation of overlay zoning districts, and Sections 609 and 610 of the Municipalities 
Planning Code establish the procedures for enactment of zoning ordinance amendments, which 
procedures have been followed, including review by the Borough Planning Commission and the Berks 
County Planning Commission and a duly advertised public hearing; and 
WHEREAS, Borough Council finds that this Ordinance is consistent with the Borough 
Comprehensive Plan and will promote the public health, safety, and general welfare of the residents 
and inhabitants of the Borough. 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT ORDAINED AND ENACTED by the Borough Council of 
the Borough of Wyomissing, Berks County, Pennsylvania, as follows: 
SECTION 1. Chapter 27 (Zoning), Part 5- A, Commercial and Industrial Redevelopment Overlay 
District, of the Code of Ordinances of the Borough of Wyomissing is hereby repealed and restated in 
its entirety to read as set forth in Exhibit “A” attached hereto and incorporated herein by reference. 
DRAFT

SECTION 2. The Official Zoning Map of the Borough of Wyomissing is hereby amended to revise 
the boundaries of the Commercial and Industrial Redevelopment Overlay District as described in 
§501A.5 of Exhibit “A” and as depicted on the map attached hereto as Exhibit “B”, so that the overlay 
district includes, without limitation, the entirety of the properties identified on Exhibit “B”. 
SECTION 3. Severability. If any sentence, clause, section, or part of this Ordinance is for any reason 
found to be unconstitutional, illegal, or invalid, such unconstitutionality, illegality, or invalidity shall 
not affect or impair any of the remaining provisions, sentences, clauses, sections, or parts of this 
Ordinance, it being the intent of Borough Council that this Ordinance would have been adopted had 
such unconstitutional, illegal, or invalid provision not been included herein. 
SECTION 4. Repealer. All ordinances or parts of ordinances inconsistent with this Ordinance are 
hereby repealed to the extent of such inconsistency. 
SECTION 5. Effective Date. This Ordinance shall become effective at the earliest date permitted by 
law.  
SECTION 6.  Code of Ordinances .  The Code of Ordinances, as amended, of the Borough of 
Wyomissing, Berks County, Pennsylvania shall be and remain unchanged and in full force and effect 
except as amended, supplemented, and modified by this Ordinance.  This Ordinance shall become part 
of this Code of Ordinances upon adoption. 
DULY ORDAINED and ENACTED as an Ordinance this         day of           , 2026.

DRAFT

EXHIBIT “A” 
CHAPTER 27 – ZONING 
PART 5-A 
COMMERCIAL AND INDUSTRIAL REDEVELOPMENT OVERLAY DISTRICT 
(Repealed and Restated) 
§501A. Specific Intent. 
1. In addition to the conventional commercial and industrial zoning districts established by this 
Chapter, there shall be established a Commercial and Industrial Redevelopment Overlay District 
(the “Overlay District”). 
2. The Overlay District shall not be an independent zoning district, but shall be a set of special 
zoning regulations to be applied over and above the zoning regulations otherwise controlling upon 
a tract of land, as enabled by and described in Section 605  of the Pennsylvania Municipalities 
Planning Code. The district upon which the Overlay District is superimposed is referred to herein 
as the “underlying district.” 
3. The purpose of the Overlay District is to provide standards for the comprehensive 
redevelopment of aging commercial and industrial sites within the Borough of Wyomissing, by: 
(A) permitting certain uses or combinations of uses not otherwise permitted in  the underlying 
district; (B) permitting a greater density and intensity of development than would otherwise be 
permitted in the underlying district; (C) permitting the fee -simple subdivision of individual 
buildings and building pads within a unified redev elopment site in the manner of a planned unit 
development; (D) permitting shared parking, shared access, shared stormwater management, and 
shared utility infrastructure among multiple lots under separate ownership, administered by a 
mandatory lot owners as sociation; (E) providing reductions in required off -street parking in 
exchange for bicycle and pedestrian improvements that implement the Official Bike Route Map 
of the Borough of Wyomissing and the Borough of West Reading as well as other improvements; 
and (F) requiring enhanced greenspace, shade trees, and landscaping within parking areas, 
consistent with §604 of this Chapter, and streetscape improvements along adjacent public streets, 
so as to promote the public health, safety, and general welfare of the  residents and inhabitants of 
the Borough. 
4. A secondary purpose of the Overlay District is to encourage respect for the historic and 
architectural identity of the structures and neighborhoods in which redevelopment may occur 
under the terms of this Part, and to promote walkable, connected development that reduces reliance 
on automobiles. 
5. The Overlay District shall be available throughout the area depicted as such on the Zoning Map 
of the Borough of Wyomissing.  Where the boundary of the Overlay District divides a single lot, 
the entire lot shall be deemed to be within the Overlay District. A site is eligible for redevelopment 
under this Part at an applicant’s election provided the following requirements are met: 
DRAFT

A. A site proposed for redevelopment shall be under single ownership, or in the case of 
multiple ownership, the lot or lots shall be redeveloped according to a single overall plan 
(the “Master Redevelopment Plan”). 
B. The site proposed for redevelopment shall be a minimum of one (1) acre in area. If the 
site is not a single, contiguous parcel, no part of the site shall be separated by more than 
one hundred (100) feet from another part of the site. If a site containing a minimum of one 
(1) acre is approved under this Section, or was approved under a previously enacted version 
of a regulation on the same topic, smaller adjacent properties may qualify under this 
Section at a later date as long as the smaller adjacent pro perties are within the boundaries 
of the Overlay District and the other requirements of this Section (other than minimum site 
acreage) are met. 
C. The site proposed for redevelopment shall be occupied by existing commercial or 
industrial development, and the following shall apply: (1) the majority of the existing 
commercial or industrial development was originally constructed at least thirty (30) years 
prior to the proposed redevelopment; and (2) due to changing economic or technological 
conditions, the existing commercial or industrial development is no longer appropriate. 
D. The site is not required to be located within a single underlying zoning district, but all 
portions of the site which are planned to be redeveloped in accordance with this Part shall 
be located within a commercial or industrial zoning district or within fifty (50) feet of such 
a district. 
E. Land may be added to an established redevelopment site, provided the revised 
redevelopment site, as modified by the addition of land, continues to meet the requirements 
of this Part. 
F. Plans for redevelopment shall demonstrate, to the extent practically achievable, respect 
for the historic and architectural identity of the structures and neighborhoods in which 
redevelopment may occur under the terms of this Part  by id entifying in the Master 
Redevelopment Plan any resource shown on Figure 13 of the Suburban Berks West Joint 
Comprehensive Plan that is on or adjacent to the redevelopment site, along with a narrative 
as to how the redevelopment relates to such resource and the adjoining neighborhood.. 
G. Plans for redevelopment shall include a schedule for completion of the development, 
which schedule shall be subject to the approval of the Borough Council. 
6. To the extent any regulation in this Part conflicts with any regulation in any other Part of this 
Chapter, the regulation in this Part shall be controlling. 
§502A. Uses Permitted by Right. 
Land and buildings in the Overlay District may be used for any one (1) or combination of the 
following purposes and no others, unless a special exception as provided in §503A or Section 553 
is granted: 
DRAFT

A. All uses and accessory uses permitted by right in any of the underlying districts which 
comprise the site. 
B. The following commercial uses, whether or not permitted in the underlying district: 
(1) Shopping center, retail store, and retail service establishment, including a 
department store, discount department store, free-standing discount superstore, and 
warehouse club, with no maximum gross floor area per establishment; 
(2) Grocery store, supermarket, and specialty food store; 
(3) Restaurant, café, coffee shop, and similar eating and drinking establishment, 
including such an establishment with a drive -through window, provided that 
stacking lanes shall be designed so that queued vehicles do not obstruct any parking 
aisle, driveway intersection, or pedestrian walkway; 
(4) Convenience store, with or without the retail sale of gasoline or other motor 
vehicle fuels, and automobile fuel or service station, provided that all fueling 
positions, canopies, and underground storage tanks comply with applicable federal 
and state r egulations and are located at least one hundred (100) feet from any 
primary residential structure; 
(5) Medical office, medical clinic, walk-in clinic, urgent care facility, and outpatient 
medical facility; 
(6) Health and fitness club, gymnasium, and indoor recreation facility; 
(7) Business and professional offices, financial institutions with or without drive -
through facilities, and hotels; 
(8) Entertainment uses, including theaters and indoor amusement establishments; 
and 
(9) Multiple principal uses and multiple principal buildings upon a single lot, and 
any combination of the uses listed in this subsection within a single building. 
(10) Accessory storage for warehousing and off -site distributing of manufactured 
foods, goods, and materials shall be permitted as an accessory use provided that (1) 
the accessory storage is located in the same structure as the principal use and (2) 
said storage does not exceed twenty five (25) percent of the gross floor area of the 
structure where the principal and accessory use is located. 
C. Development containing apartments and/or townhouses, above one or more non -
residential uses permitted in any of the underlying districts which comprise the site, subject 
to the following: 
(1) No new residential use under this §502A.C shall be allowed on the first floor 
level of any property in the Overlay District. 
DRAFT

(2) Residential uses by right under this §502A.C shall not occupy more than thirty 
(30) percent of the combined area available for occupation within the 
redevelopment site. 
(3) Notwithstanding paragraph (2) above, development containing up to one 
hundred (100) percent apartments and/or townhouses shall be permitted without 
regard to the percentage of the combined area available for occupation where all of 
the following are satisfied: (a) the redevelopment site includes only a single existing 
building; (b) the existing building is being renovated rather than demolished and 
replaced; (c) the redevelopment site is under five (5) acres in area; (d) the 
redevelopment site is adjacent to a ten (10) acre or greater mixed use redevelopment 
site previously approved under this Part 5 -A; and (e) pedestrian circulation in and 
through the redevelopment site is integrated with adjacent and adjoining existing 
or planned development in accorda nce with a sidewalk and street light plan 
acceptable to the Borough. 
(4) No single building may include a use under this §502A.C together with a use 
under §502A.D. Where uses under this §502A.C and §502A.D occur within a single 
redevelopment site, the uses shall be located within separate buildings. 
D. Development containing apartments and/or townhouses intended to be “55 or Over 
Housing” within the meaning of the Fair Housing Act (42 U.S.C.A. §3601 et seq.), so as 
to qualify as “housing for older persons” within the meaning of the Fair Housing Act, above 
one or more non -residential uses permitted in any of the underlying districts which 
comprise the site, subject to the following: 
(1) No new residential use under this §502A.D shall be allowed on the first floor 
level of any property in the Overlay District. 
(2) The restriction shall be subject to all applicable Federal and State laws 
concerning “housing for older persons.” Occupancy of any dwelling unit shall be 
restricted to persons fifty-five (55) years of age or older (“age qualified”), provided, 
however, that a person who is age qualified may occupy such unit with such 
person’s spouse, regardless of age, and with a child nineteen (19) years of age or 
older who is not enrolled in a secondary school. No occupancy shall be permitted 
by any person under the age of nineteen (19) unless such person is a handicapped 
dependent p rotected by the provisions of the Fair Housing Act. A permanent 
resident is a person who resides in a dwelling unit for ninety (90) or more 
consecutive days. Permitted visitations shall not exceed ninety (90) consecutive 
days. 
(3) Accompanying the plan for redevelopment shall be a declaration of restrictive 
covenants (the “Age Restriction Declaration”) which must be approved by the 
Borough Council and recorded by the developer. The Age Restriction Declaration’s 
restrictions shall insure that the proposed development will function as depicted on 
DRAFT

the plan for redevelopment and be in compliance with the Federal Fair Housing Act 
Amendments of 1988, or as subsequently amended. The following are the minimum 
requirements for the Age Restriction Declaration: 
(a) Residency qualifications applicable to the development shall be included 
in the Age Restriction Declaration. Residents shall be limited by deed and 
lease to households including one permanent resident age fifty -five (55) 
years or older, and permanent o ccupancy by any person age nineteen (19) 
or younger shall be prohibited, except as follows: (i) individual units may 
occasionally house persons younger than nineteen (19) years of age, such 
as grandchildren, provided they reside within the unit for less th an ninety 
(90) days in any calendar year; or (ii) members of a household shall not be 
required to vacate a dwelling unit if a resident age fifty -five (55) or older 
leaves the household due to death, divorce, or placement in a nursing or 
assisted care facility and the remaining household members would qualify 
for residency if the departed resident were still present in the household. 
(b) The applicant shall prove to the satisfaction of the Borough Council that 
an appropriate entity will have authority to assure compliance with the age 
limitation. 
(4) Residential uses by right under this §502A.D shall not occupy more than eighty 
(80) percent of the combined area available for occupation within the 
redevelopment site. Where uses under §502A.C and this §502A.D are combined 
within a single redevelopmen t site, the eighty (80) percent combined area 
regulation under this paragraph shall apply to the sum of the areas of the two uses, 
but in no event shall the use under §502A.C exceed thirty (30) percent of the total 
combined area available for occupation within the redevelopment site. 
(5) No single building may include a use under §502A.C or §503A.B together with 
a use under this §502A.D. Where such uses occur within a single redevelopment 
site, the uses shall be located within separate buildings. 
E. Any combination of the above uses, subject to the limitations set forth in this §502A. 
§503A. Uses Permitted by Special Exception. 
The following uses are permitted when special exceptions are granted by the Zoning Hearing 
Board in accordance with the applicable provisions of this Part: 
A. All uses and accessory uses permitted by special exception in any of the underlying 
commercial or industrial districts which comprise the site, excluding the adult uses listed 
in Section 553(A). 
B. Development containing apartments and/or townhouses above one or more non -
residential uses permitted in any of the underlying commercial or industrial districts which 
comprise the redevelopment site, subject to the following: 
DRAFT

(1) No new residential use under this §503A.B shall be allowed on the first floor 
level of any property in the Overlay District. 
(2) Residential uses under §502A.C and this §503A.B, together, shall not occupy 
more than sixty (60) percent of the combined area available for occupation within 
the redevelopment site. 
(3) Where uses under §502A.C and this §503A.B are proposed to occur along with 
the use under §502A.D, the eighty (80) percent combined area regulation under 
§502A.D(4) shall apply to the sum of the areas of the three uses, but in no event 
shall the use under §502A.C and the use under this §503A.B, together, exceed sixty 
(60) percent of the total combined area available for occupation within the 
redevelopment site. 
(4) No single building may include a use under §502A.C or this §503A.B together 
with a use under §502A.D. Where such uses occur within a single redevelopment 
site, the uses shall be located within separate buildings. 
§504A. Area, Yard and Height Requirements. 
The following requirements shall apply within the overall redevelopment site. Where lots are 
created by fee-simple subdivision pursuant to §505A, compliance shall be measured against the 
overall redevelopment site as provided in §505A.3, and not against each individual lot. 
Maximum Permitted:

Building Height 75 feet 
Building Coverage 60% within the overall redevelopment site 
Impervious Area 75% within the overall redevelopment site, subject to 
any exclusions provided for herein

interior lots created by fee-simple subdivision 
Building Setback Underlying district setback, except; 0 feet where the 
building setback line is along the boundary of 
another lot within the redevelopment site

side yard is along the boundary of another lot within 
the redevelopment site

Standard Minimum 
Rear Yard Underlying district setback, except; 0 feet where the 
rear yard is along the boundary of another lot within 
the redevelopment site 
Lot Width at Street Line 100 feet, except as provided in §505A for interior 
lots 
Lot Width at Building Setback Line 100 feet, except as provided in §505A for interior 
lots 
Open Area 25% within the overall redevelopment site 
 
For purposes of this Section, Open Area is ground upon which no principal or accessory buildings, 
structures, or uses and paved area are permitted; provided, however, notwithstanding anything in 
this Part to the contrary, Open Area shall include any area o f a lot, including the area above a 
building or structure, covered by grass or other permeable landscape material (i.e. green roof), 
such as, but not limited to, mulch, gravel, landscape stone, landscaping pavers, or park benches; 
and provided further, at least 5% of the overall redevelopment site shall consist of Open Area 
exclusive of any area above a building or structure. Landscaped areas within parking lots provided 
pursuant to §507A shall count toward the Open Area requirement. Open Area that includes the 
area above a building or structure, covered by grass or other permeable landscape material shall 
be considered pervious area for the purpose of calculating Impervious Area. 
§505A. Fee-Simple (Planned Unit Development Style) Subdivision; Financial Subdivision. 
1. Purpose. It is the intent of this Section to permit, within an approved redevelopment site, the 
division of land into individual fee -simple lots corresponding to individual buildings, building 
pads, or portions of the site, including divisions made for legal, financing, taxation, or accounting 
purposes without physical separation of buildings or site improvements (a “financial subdivision”), 
while requiring that the redevelopment site continue to function, and be regulated, as a single 
unified development. 
2. Subdivision Permitted. Notwithstanding any contrary provision of this Chapter , land within an 
approved redevelopment site may be subdivided into fee -simple lots, and buildings may be 
conveyed on separate lots, subject to the following: 
A. Lot lines may be drawn at or around the perimeter of a building or building pad, along 
the face of a building wall, or through a party wall separating attached buildings or attached 
units within a building; 
B. Interior lots (lots which do not abut a public street) shall be permitted, and no minimum 
lot size, minimum lot width, minimum street frontage, minimum yard, or maximum 
building coverage requirement shall apply to any individual interior lot, provided that each 
such lot has guaranteed vehicular and pedestrian access to a public street by way of 
recorded easement over the common driveways and access ways of the redevelopment site; 
DRAFT

C. Zero (0) foot setbacks shall be permitted along any lot line internal to the redevelopment 
site; and 
D. Off-street parking, loading, stormwater management facilities, signage, landscaping, 
and open space serving any lot may be located on any other lot within the redevelopment 
site, provided such facilities are secured by recorded cross-easements and are subject to the 
Lot Owners Association documents required by §511A. 
3. Unified Compliance. Compliance with the use, area, bulk, coverage, open area, parking, 
landscaping, stormwater, and all other requirements of this Part shall be determined on the basis 
of the overall redevelopment site as shown on the approved Master Redevelopment Plan, and not 
on the basis of individual lots. No subdivision, conveyance, or lease of any lot or building shall 
create or increase any nonconformity with respect to the overall redevelopment site, and no lot 
shall be conveyed except subject to the Declaration required by §511A. 
4. Required Documents. As a condition of final approval of any subdivision under this Section, 
the applicant shall record, in form approved by the Borough Solicitor: (A) a Declaration of 
Covenants, Conditions, Restrictions and Easements (the “Declaration”)  satisfying §511A; (B) 
reciprocal easement agreements (or equivalent provisions within the Declaration) providing 
perpetual cross-access, cross-parking, utility, drainage, stormwater, construction, and maintenance 
easements benefitting and burdening every lot within the redevelopment site; and (C) a plan note 
on the record plan stating that the lots shown thereon are part of a unified redevelopment site under 
Part 5-A of the Borough Zoning Ordinance and may not be developed, used, or conveyed except 
in accordance with the approved Master Redevelopment Plan and the Declaration. 
5. Subsequent Changes. Following recording, individual lots may be further subdivided, 
consolidated, or reconfigured, and lot lines may be adjusted, by revised subdivision plan, provided 
the overall redevelopment site remains in compliance with this Part and the Declaration is amended 
as necessary. 
§506A. Off -Street Parking; Shared Parking; Bicycle and Pedestrian Improvement 
Incentives. 
1. Base Parking Requirements. The following off -street parking requirements shall apply to the 
listed uses within the Overlay District. For any use not listed, the requirement of Part 6 of this 
Chapter shall apply. 
Use Required Off-Street Parking 
Townhouses 2 spaces for each unit 
Apartments 1.5 spaces for each unit 
Office 1 space for each 250 square feet of gross floor area 
Medical office / clinic 1 space for each 250 square feet of gross floor area, 
or 5 spaces per doctor plus 1 space per employee, 
whichever the applicant elects 
DRAFT

Use Required Off-Street Parking 
Hotel 1 space per unit 
Restaurant / café 1 space per 3 seats plus 1 space for each employee on 
the shift of greatest employment 
Retail / service / shopping center / 
grocery / discount superstore 
1 space per each 300 square feet of gross floor area 
Health / fitness club 1 space per each 300 square feet of gross floor area 
Convenience store with fuel sales 1 space per each 300 square feet of gross floor area 
plus 1 space per fueling position; fueling positions 
may be counted as parking spaces for this purpose 
 
2. Floor area devoted exclusively to storage, mechanical equipment, or similar non -occupiable 
purposes which require no additional employees, as objectively demonstrated to the satisfaction 
of the Zoning Officer, for the use shall be excluded from gross floor area for purposes of computing 
required parking, as determined by the Zoning Officer. 
3. Shared Parking Between Lots. Uses within the overall redevelopment site may share off -street 
parking spaces, including spaces located on other lots under different ownership within the 
redevelopment site, where the combination of uses is compatible in t erms of days and hours of 
usage. The minimum number of off -street parking spaces which shall be provided within the 
overall redevelopment site for the combination of uses shall be computed in accordance with 
paragraphs A through F of this subsection 3 and the shared parking methodology set forth in Shared 
Parking, Third Edition (2020), published by the Urban Land Institute, the National Parking 
Association, and the International Council of Shopping Centers, as amended from time to time, 
including by any subsequent edition (the “ULI Shared Parking Methodology”). All shared spaces 
shall be secured by recorded cross-parking easements and shall be maintained by the Lot Owners 
Association pursuant to §511A. No shared space shall be reserved, gated, or otherwise r estricted 
to a single use or lot except as shown on the approved Master Redevelopment Plan. 
A. Base Requirement by Use. The number of off -street parking spaces required for each 
use shall first be determined on an individual basis under subsection 1 or, for any use not 
listed in subsection 1, under Part 6 of this Chapter. 
B. Adjustment Factors. The number of spaces so determined for each use shall be 
multiplied by the time-of-day adjustment factor and the monthly adjustment factor assigned 
to that use, or to the corresponding land use category, under the ULI Shared Parking 
Methodology, for each hour of a weekday and of a weekend day in each month of the year. 
C. Minimum Supply. The adjusted figures for all uses shall be totaled for each such hour. 
The greatest total for any hour shall be the minimum number of off -street parking spaces 
which shall be provided within the overall redevelopment site for the combination of uses. 
DRAFT

D. Uses Not Addressed. Where the ULI Shared Parking Methodology does not assign 
adjustment factors to a use or to a corresponding land use category, the applicant shall 
apply the factors for the most comparable land use category, as approved by the Zoning 
Officer in consultation with the Borough Engineer. Absent such approval, the use shall be 
computed at one hundred percent (100%) of its individual requirement in every hour and 
month. 
E. Other Adjustments. Adjustments under the ULI Shared Parking Methodology for non -
captive patronage, driving ratios, or modal split shall not be applied in the computation 
under this subsection 3, but may be considered as part of a Parking Demand Analysis under 
subsection 4. 
F. Submission; Edition Applied. The shared parking computation shall be prepared by a 
qualified professional traffic engineer, shall be submitted with the Master Redevelopment 
Plan, and shall apply the edition of the ULI Shared Parking Methodology in effec t on the 
date the application for approval of the Master Redevelopment Plan is filed. 
4. Alternative Parking Demand Analysis. In lieu of or in addition to the computations under 
subsections 1 and 3, the Borough Council may approve a parking supply based upon a parking 
demand analysis prepared by a qualified professional traffic engineer utilizing site-specific parking 
counts and/or the parking generation methodologies of the Institute of Transportation Engineers 
(ITE), current edition, and/or the ULI Shared Parking Methodology, including a time -of-day 
shared parking analysis (a “Parking Demand Analysis”). The approved Parking Demand Analysis 
for a redevelopment site shall establish the minimum parking floor for that site under subsection 
5.D below. 
5. Parking Reductions for Bicycle and Pedestrian Improvements. It is the express intent of this Part 
to permit the reduction of required off -street parking in exchange for bicycle and pedestrian 
improvements. The number of off-street parking spaces otherwise required under this Section shall 
be reduced by the following credits, subject to the limitations of subsection 5.D: 
A. Credits. The following credits may be combined (said credits are also reflected in the 
table set forth below): 
(1) Bicycle parking and support facilities: five (5) required automobile spaces 
waived for each installed and maintained rack, locker, bicycle room, or other secure 
facility accommodating at least four (4) bicycles; five (5) required automobile 
spaces waiv ed for each installed and maintained public bicycle repair station 
equipped with, at a minimum, a fixed tool array and an air pump; and five (5) 
required automobile spaces waived for each installed and maintained bus stop 
shelter; in each case up to a maxi mum aggregate reduction under this subsection 
(1) of three percent (3%) of the base requirement; 
(2) Internal pedestrian network: a reduction of five percent (5%) of the base 
requirement where a continuous internal sidewalk and crosswalk network at least 
five (5) feet in width connects every principal building entrance to every other 
DRAFT

principal building, to all parking areas serving the building, and to the perimeter 
public sidewalk system, with raised or high -visibility crosswalks at all driveway 
and aisle crossings; 
(3) Perimeter and off-site connections: a reduction of two percent (2%) of the base 
requirement for each publicly accessible pedestrian or bicycle connection 
constructed by the applicant linking the redevelopment site to an adjoining public 
street, adjoining residential neighborhood, transit stop, or adjoining trail or planned 
trail (including sidewalks and multi -use paths along State Hill Road, Woodland 
Road, and Paper Mill Road, coordinated with PennDOT where applicable), up to a 
maximum reduction of eigh t percent (8%). To qualify for a credit under this 
subsection (3), a connection shall connect to, or extend, a bicycle route, trail, 
pedestrian facility, or transit stop shown on the Official Bike Route Map of the 
Borough of Wyomissing and the Borough of W est Reading, as adopted by 
ordinance of Borough Council and as thereafter amended (the “Official Bike Route 
Map”), or shall be identified as a priority connection by the Borough Planning 
Commission. All connections constructed under this subsection shall b e designed 
to be consistent with the Official Bike Route Map; 
(4) Multi-use path or bicycle lane: a reduction of five percent (5%) of the base 
requirement where the applicant constructs a multi-use path at least ten (10) feet in 
width, or dedicated bicycle lanes, along the full frontage of the redevelopment site 
on at least one abutting arterial or collector road; 
(5) Pedestrian amenities: a reduction of up to three percent (3%) of the base 
requirement, in the discretion of Borough Council, for pedestrian -scale lighting, 
benches, shade structures, bus shelters, and similar amenities installed along the 
internal pedestrian network; 
(6) Electric vehicle and micromobility infrastructure: a reduction of one (1) space 
for each two (2) installed electric vehicle charging spaces and for each shared 
micromobility (bicycle or scooter share) station, up to a maximum reduction of two 
percent (2%) of the base requirement; and 
(7) Ride-share passenger loading facilities: three (3) required automobile spaces 
waived for each installed and maintained pick-up and drop-off stall within a Ride-
Share Loading Zone meeting subsection 5.F up to a maximum aggregate reduction 
under this subsection (7) of two percent (2%) of the base requirement. 
B. All improvements for which a credit is claimed shall be shown on the Master 
Redevelopment Plan, shall be constructed prior to or concurrently with the phase of 
development they serve, and shall be perpetually maintained by the Lot Owners 
Association. Fa ilure to maintain any such improvement shall render the corresponding 
parking reduction void, and the Borough may require restoration of the improvement or 
provision of the waived parking. 
DRAFT

C. Maximum Reduction. The aggregate reduction under this subsection 5 shall not exceed 
twenty-five percent (25%) of the base parking requirement computed under subsections 1 
through 3. 
D. Parking Floor. Notwithstanding any other provision of this Section, in no event shall 
the total number of off -street parking spaces provided within the overall redevelopment 
site, after application of all shared parking computations and all reduction credits, be fewer 
than the peak parking demand identified in an approved Parking Demand Analysis for the 
redevelopment site. 
E. Consistency with the Official Bike Route Map. The Master Redevelopment Plan shall 
depict the redevelopment site in relation to the Official Bike Route Map, shall identify each 
bicycle route, trail, pedestrian facility, and transit stop shown on the Offi cial Bike Route 
Map which abuts the redevelopment site or lies within one -half (1/2) mile of it, and shall 
show how the bicycle and pedestrian improvements proposed under this subsection 5 
connect to those facilities. Where the Official Bike Route Map depi cts a route along or 
across the frontage of the redevelopment site, the applicant shall construct the portion of 
that route lying within or along the redevelopment site to the standards of the Official Bike 
Route Map and other applicable regulations. 
F. Ride-Share Loading Zone. A “Ride -Share Loading Zone” is an area set aside only for 
vehicles to stop briefly while passengers get in or out, including vehicles operated by 
transportation network company drivers, taxicabs, paratransit and microtransit ser vices, 
and private vehicles. A Ride -Share Loading Zone is not a parking area. To qualify for a 
credit under subsection 5.A(7), a Ride-Share Loading Zone shall: 
(1) be located within one hundred fifty (150) feet of the principal public entrance 
of the building it serves, and be connected to that entrance by the internal pedestrian 
network described in subsection 5.A(2); 
(2) be located outside of, and not obstruct, any drive aisle, fire apparatus access 
road or fire lane, drive-through stacking lane, or loading area required by §608 of 
this Chapter, and be located on the building side of any drive aisle or, where that is 
not practicable, be connected to the building by a raised or high -visibility 
crosswalk; 
(3) provide stalls not less than ten (10) feet by twenty -two (22) feet, of which at 
least one stall in every continuous one hundred (100) linear feet of the zone, or 
fraction thereof, shall be an accessible passenger loading zone meeting the 2010 
ADA Standards for Accessible Design or any successor standard; 
(4) be marked and signed for passenger pick-up and drop-off only, with no parking 
or unattended vehicles, and be identified by wayfinding signage visible from the 
building entrance served; 
DRAFT

(5) include a passenger waiting area with seating, pedestrian -scale lighting, and a 
shelter, canopy, or shade structure, provided that waiting -area amenities used to 
qualify under this paragraph shall not also be credited under subsection 5.A(1) or 
5.A(5); and 
(6) be available to all vehicles described in this subsection on a nondiscriminatory 
basis. Stalls within a Ride -Share Loading Zone shall not be counted as off -street 
parking spaces or as loading spaces under §608 of this Chapter. 
Nothing in this subsection F shall be construed to license, tax, or regulate any transportation 
network company or transportation network service. The obligations of this subsection F 
run solely to the applicant, the lot owners, and the Lot Owners Association. 
6. Parking Design. Parking stalls shall be a minimum of nine (9) feet by eighteen (18) feet with 
twenty-four (24) foot two-way aisles, except that accessible spaces, parallel spaces, and oversize 
spaces shall be dimensioned in accordance with applicable law and sound engineering practice as 
approved by the Borough Engineer.

Parking Floor (5.D).  After application of all shared parking computations and all reduction

not be fewer than the peak parking demand identified in an approved Parking Demand

§507A. Parking Area Greenspace, Shade Trees, and Landscaping. 
1. Purpose. Large expanses of unbroken pavement are contrary to the purposes of this Part. Parking 
areas within the Overlay District  that elect redevelopment under this Part  shall incorporate 
substantial interior and perimeter greenspace, shade trees, and landscaping to reduce heat island 
effect, manage stormwater, provide safe pedestrian circulation, and improve the appearance of the 
redevelopment site. 
2. Interior Landscaped Area. Not less than ten percent (10%) of the interior area of each surface 
parking lot containing twenty (20) or more spaces (measured as the area devoted to parking stalls 
and drive aisles) shall consist of landscaped islands, medians, or planting strips. Landscaped areas 
within parking lots shall count toward the Open Area requirement of §504A. 
3. Landscape Islands. A landscaped island not less than nine (9) feet in width and not less than one 
hundred sixty (160) square feet in area shall be provided: (A) at each end of each parking row; and 
DRAFT

(B) at intervals such that no more than fifteen (15) contiguous parking spaces occur in any row 
without an intervening landscaped island. Landscaped medians not less than ten (10) feet in width 
shall be provided between every fourth double row of parking, and such medians shall, wherever 
practicable, incorporate the pedestrian walkways required by §506A.5.A(2). 
4. Shade Trees. Deciduous trees shall be provided within or immediately adjacent to each surface 
parking lot at a ratio of not less than one (1) deciduous tree for each ten (10) parking spaces, any 
fractional result being rounded up to the next whole tree.  Deciduous trees located within interior 
landscaped islands shall be shade trees; deciduous trees located along the perimeter of a parking 
lot may be either shade trees or ornamental trees. Each shade tree shall be a minimum of two and 
one-half (2.5) inch caliper measured at six (6) inches above grade, shall be limbed up to six (6) 
feet in height, and shall be a species having a mature canopy spread of not less than thirty (30) feet 
selected from the recommended tree lists in §604 of this Chapter or otherwi se approved by the 
Borough. Each ornamental tree shall meet the minimum size standards of §604. Each required tree 
shall be planted in a pervious area of not less than one hundred sixty (160) square feet (or in a 
structural soil or suspended pavement system providing not less than four hundred (400) cubic feet 
of soil volume per tree), within a landscaped island or within ten (10) feet of the parking lot 
perimeter, with the center of the tree located not less than three (3) feet from the edge of any curb 
or paving and not less than ten (10) feet from any underground or overhead utility. Tree species 
shall be diversified such that no single species comprises more than twenty-five percent (25%) of 
required parking lot trees, not less than sixty -six percent (66 %) of the required trees shall be of 
species native to the northeastern United States, and no species or cultivar deemed invasive by the 
Pennsylvania Department of Conservation and Natural Resources shall be planted. The design 
shall achieve, at tree maturity, canopy coverage of not less than thirty percent (30%) of the surface 
parking area. 
5. Perimeter Landscaping. A landscaped planting strip not less than ten (10) feet in width shall be 
provided between any surface parking area and any public street right -of-way, and not less than 
fifteen (15) feet in width between any surface parking area and any residential district boundary, 
planted with a combination of shade trees, ornamental trees, shrubs, and groundcover forming an 
effective visual buffer as approved by the Borough Planning Commission. Where such planting 
strip includes a sidewalk or pedestrian or bicycle path, the planting strip shall include landscaped 
areas on either side of such sidewalk or path.  Such sidewalks,  pedestrian, or bicycle paths shall 
not count as impervious cover under Section 504A above. Where a surface parking area abuts a 
residential zoning district or an existing residential use, the planting strip shall constitute a screen 
buffer meeting §604 of this Chapter, installed at a minimum height of six (6) feet and producing, 
within three (3) years, a complete and continuous visual screen of not less than six (6) feet in 
height. Screen buffer plantings shall be fully contained between the property line and the yard 
setback line. Perimeter landscaping shall be shown on the landscape plan required by subsection 
7 and is subject to approval by the Borough Planning Commission. 
6. Stormwater Integration. Landscaped islands, medians, and perimeter strips shall, wherever 
feasible as determined by the Borough Engineer, be designed as rain gardens, bioretention areas, 
bioswales, or other vegetated stormwater best management practices  receiving runoff from 
DRAFT

adjacent pavement, and such facilities shall count toward both the landscaping requirements of 
this Section and the stormwater requirements of §509A. 
7. Landscape Plan; Maintenance. A landscape plan shall be submitted as part of a preliminary plan 
or final plan (if not preceded by a preliminary plan or zoning permit). The landscape plan shall be 
prepared by a Landscape Architect with current and active registration in the Commonwealth of 
Pennsylvania, shall meet the requirements of §604 of this Chapter, and is subject to approval by 
the Borough Planning Commission. All required landscaping and shade trees shall be perpetually 
maintained or replaced, and any plant material which is in decline or has expired shall be replaced 
in kind within six (6) months of observance, by the Lot Owners Association pursuant to §511A. 
8. Relationship to §604. The requirements of this Section are in addition to the landscaping 
requirements of §604 of this Chapter. Where a requirement of this Section and a requirement of 
§604 differ, the more restrictive requirement shall apply. Any lands cape requirement calculation 
under this Section which results in a fraction shall be rounded up to a whole plant. 
§508A.  Recreation Dedication. 
1. Applicability. For each redevelopment project under this Part which includes dwelling units, the 
applicant shall satisfy the recreation land dedication and fee requirements of §509.1 of the Borough 
Subdivision and Land Development Ordinance. 
2. Open space provided under this Section shall count toward the Open Area requirement of 
§504A, and publicly accessible plazas, greens, seating areas, and trail corridors within the 
redevelopment site may be credited toward this requirement in the discretion of Borough Council. 
§509A. Shared Stormwater Management. 
1. Redevelopment under this Part shall comply with the Borough Stormwater Management 
Ordinance and all applicable state and federal requirements, including NPDES permitting. 
2. Shared Facilities Permitted. Stormwater management facilities — including detention and 
retention basins, subsurface storage, rain gardens, bioretention areas, water quality devices, 
conveyance systems, and outfalls — may be designed, constructed, owned, and maintained on a 
shared basis serving multiple lots and multiple buildings within the redevelopment site, 
notwithstanding that such lots are under different ownership, and may be located on any lot within 
the redevelopment site. Rate, volume, and water quality compliance shall be computed on the basis 
of the overall redevelopment site. 
3. Easements and Responsibility. All shared stormwater facilities shall be located within recorded 
drainage and access easements benefitting each lot they serve and the Borough. The Lot Owners 
Association shall own or control, and shall be solely responsib le for the inspection, operation, 
maintenance, repair, and replacement of, all shared stormwater facilities, pursuant to a recorded 
Stormwater Management Facilities Operation and Maintenance Agreement in form approved by 
the Borough Solicitor and Borough E ngineer, which agreement shall: (A) run with the land and 
bind all present and future lot owners; (B) grant the Borough the right, but not the obligation, 
following notice and opportunity to cure, to enter upon the site, perform required maintenance, 
DRAFT

and lien each lot within the redevelopment site for its proportionate share of the cost; (C) require 
routine inspection and reporting to the Borough at intervals specified by the Borough Engineer; 
and (D) require the Association to maintain a dedicated reserve for stormwater facility repair and 
replacement. 
§510A. Utility Infrastructure. 
1. All new and relocated electric, telecommunications, and cable service lines within the 
redevelopment site shall be placed underground, except for transformers, switchgear, and similar 
at-grade appurtenances, which shall be screened by landscaping or architectural features. Existing 
electric utility service areas may remain and be upgraded in place. 
2. Shared private utility infrastructure — including private water and sanitary sewer laterals and 
mains, fire loops and hydrants, stormwater conveyance, site lighting, common signage electrical 
service, and communications conduit — serving more than one lot or building shall be permitted, 
shall be located within recorded utility easements benefitting each lot served, and shall be owned 
or controlled, operated, maintained, repaired, and replaced by the Lot Owners Association 
pursuant to §511A, except to the extent such facilities are accepted for dedication by the applicable 
public utility, authority, or the Borough. 
3. Public and Private Demarcation. The Master Redevelopment Plan shall clearly identify, for each 
utility, the point at which public facilities end and private facilities begin, as follows: (A) for water 
service, the Borough or the applicable authority or utility shall own, operate, and maintain the 
public main and the master meter, and all water distribution piping, valves, hydrants, submeters, 
laterals, and appurtenances located downstream of the master meter shall be private; (B) for 
sanitary sewer service, the Borough or the applicable authority shall own, operate, and maintain 
the public sewer main, and all collection piping, laterals, cleanouts, manholes, and appurtenances 
located upstream of the point of connection to the public main shall be private; and (C) all facilities 
identified as private shall be owned or controlled, operated, maintained, repaired, and replaced by 
the Lot Owners Association pursuant to §511A, and shall not be accepted for dedication by the 
Borough or any authority except by express written agreement. Where a master meter serves more 
than one lot or building, the Declaration shall provide for submetering or for equitable allocation 
of the metered charges among the lots and buildings served. 
4. The Master Redevelopment Plan shall include a composite utility plan demonstrating adequate 
capacity, metering arrangements, and allocation of maintenance responsibility for all shared utility 
infrastructure, subject to approval by the Borough Engineer and the applicable utility providers. 
Adequate capacity shall be demonstrated by written confirmation from each applicable utility 
provider or authority. 
§511A. Lot Owners Association. 
1. Association Required. Prior to or concurrently with the recording of any subdivision plan 
creating more than one lot within a redevelopment site, or the conveyance of any portion of a 
redevelopment site, the applicant shall form a lot owners association, business owners association, 
or functionally equivalent entity organized under Pennsylvania law (the “Association”), and shall 
DRAFT

record the Declaration establishing the Association and governing the redevelopment site. The 
Declaration and the Association’s organizational documents shall be subject to review and 
approval by the Borough Solicitor for compliance with this Section. 
2. Mandatory Membership; Covenants Running with the Land. Every owner of a lot within the 
redevelopment site shall automatically be a member of the Association. The Declaration shall run 
with the land, shall bind all present and future owners, occupants, a nd mortgagees, and shall not 
be amended in any manner affecting the obligations described in this Section, nor shall the 
Association be dissolved, without the prior written consent of Borough Council. 
3. Required Responsibilities. The Declaration shall obligate the Association, at a minimum, to own 
or control and to perpetually operate, inspect, maintain, repair, replace, insure, and administer: 
A. All shared stormwater management facilities, in accordance with §509A and the 
recorded Stormwater Management Facilities Operation and Maintenance Agreement; 
B. All shared private utility infrastructure described in §510A, including private water, 
sanitary sewer, fire protection, storm sewer, site lighting, and communications facilities 
not dedicated to a public utility or authority; 
C. All shared parking areas, drive aisles, access driveways, service drives, and loading 
areas, including pavement maintenance, restriping, snow and ice removal, and enforcement 
of the shared parking arrangements approved under §506A; 
D. All common landscaping, parking lot greenspace, shade trees, buffer plantings, and open 
space areas required under §§507A and 508A, including replacement of dead or diseased 
material; 
E. All internal sidewalks, crosswalks, multi -use paths, bicycle parking, transit amenities, 
ride-share loading zones and their passenger waiting areas, and other improvements for 
which a parking reduction credit was granted under §506A.5, and all streetsca pe 
improvements required under §512A.6; 
F. Common signage, common site lighting, and common refuse and recycling facilities; 
and 
G. Any other common elements identified on the Master Redevelopment Plan. 
4. Assessments and Reserves. The Declaration shall grant the Association the power to levy 
assessments against each lot sufficient to fund the responsibilities described in subsection 3, shall 
provide that unpaid assessments constitute a lien on the delinq uent lot, and shall require the 
Association to maintain (A) an operating budget adopted annually and (B) a capital reserve fund, 
funded at a level reasonably sufficient, based on a reserve study updated not less than every five 
(5) years, for the repair an d replacement of shared stormwater facilities, shared utility 
infrastructure, and shared pavement. 
DRAFT

5. Borough Enforcement Rights. The Declaration shall name the Borough as a third -party 
beneficiary with the right, but not the obligation, to enforce the Association’s obligations under 
this Section. If the Association fails to perform any obligation relating to stormwater management, 
utility infrastructure, life safety, or required landscaping after thirty (30) days’ written notice (or 
immediately in the event of an emergency), the Borough may perform such obligation and recover 
its costs, together with re asonable attorneys’ fees and an administrative charge, from the 
Association, and may lien each lot within the redevelopment site for its proportionate share thereof, 
in the manner provided for municipal claims. 
6. Reporting. The Association shall provide to the Borough, upon request and in any event not less 
than annually, evidence of its continued good standing, its current budget and reserve balances, its 
stormwater inspection reports, and the name and contact information of its manager or responsible 
officer. 
§512A. Additional Performance Standards. 
1. The horizontal distance in feet at the closest place between any principal buildings on the same 
lot shall not be less than twenty -five (25) feet, provided that buildings separated by a lot line 
created under §505A, and attached buildings, shall be exempt from this requirement. 
2. Wall Signs and Freestanding Signs. Wall signs and freestanding signs shall be permitted in 
accordance with §606C, except that the following provisions shall apply: 
A. Freestanding Signs shall not exceed twenty-five feet in height and shall not exceed 100 
sq. ft. of gross sign area per face. Freestanding signs shall not have more than 2 faces. No 
more than one freestanding sign shall be permitted for each 1,500 feet o f street frontage 
which the freestanding sign abuts. 
B. The total sign area limitation of §606C.2 shall not apply. 
C. Within the redevelopment site, a sign attached to a building wall is not required to face 
a public street. Said signs shall be subject to the requirement that the sign shall not have an 
area exceeding 10% of the building wall to which it is attached, or two square feet of area 
for each one foot of building height plus two square feet of area for each one foot of 
building length of the wall to which it is attached, whichever is less. 
D. Signs attached to a building wall shall not exceed 100 square feet for each separate use 
within the redevelopment site (not including the freestanding sign area). 
3. Directional Signs. Within the overall redevelopment site, one (1) sign facing each direction of 
travel, not exceeding six (6) square feet in area, is permitted at each street intersection and at each 
driveway intersection, outside the legal right-of-way, to identify the redevelopment site and to give 
directions. These signs are in addition to all other signs permitted under this Chapter and do not 
count toward any sign area limitation of §606C. 
4. When a part of the overall redevelopment site is separated from another by no more than one 
hundred (100) feet in accordance with §501A.5.B: (A) the minimum distance between the 
DRAFT

centerline of a driveway where it intersects a street and the centerline of another driveway on the 
same lot or another lot (including another lot which is outside of the overall redevelopment site) 
where that other driveway intersects the same street shal l be one hundred (100) feet; (B) the 
centerline of a driveway where it intersects a street shall be a minimum of seventy -five (75) feet 
from a lot line, provided that it may be less than seventy -five (75) feet from a lot line if it will be 
at least one hun dred (100) feet from the nearest driveway on the adjoining lot (including an 
adjoining lot which is outside of the overall redevelopment site); and (C) the minimum distance 
between the centerline of a driveway where it intersects a street and the intersect ion of street 
cartway lines (except in the case of a street intersecting the street the driveway intersects directly 
opposite from the driveway) shall be one hundred (100) feet. The driveway locations and spacing 
proposed under this subsection shall be supported by a traffic impact study prepared by a qualified 
professional traffic engineer and submitted with the Master Redevelopment Plan, addressing 
driveway location and spacing, sight distance, internal circulation, and, where applicable, 
Pennsylvania Department of Transportation Highway Occupancy Permit requirements. Borough 
Council, upon the recommendation of the Borough Engineer or the Borough’s traffic engineer, 
may require greater spacing than specified in this subsection where necessary for traffic safety. 
5. Improvements within or affecting the right-of-way of State Hill Road (SR 3055), Route 422 (SR 
0422), or any other state highway, including sidewalks, multi -use paths, traffic signals, and 
corridor improvements, shall be coordinated with and subject to the approval of the Pennsylvania 
Department of Transportation. 
6. Streetscape Improvements. Streetscape improvements shall be provided along the entire 
frontage of the redevelopment site on each public street adjacent to the redevelopment site, within 
or immediately adjacent to the street right-of-way, in the locations shown on the approved Master 
Redevelopment Plan, as follows: 
A. Street Lights. Street lights meeting the Borough standard shall be installed along each 
such public street frontage, at spacing approved by the Borough Engineer. Each street light 
shall consist of a decorative pole and luminaire matching, in pole design, height, color, and 
luminaire style, the decorative street lights installed along Park Road and Penn Avenue, or 
a Borough -approved equivalent. All street lights installed under this subsection shall 
comply with §605 of this Chapter. 
B. Street Trees. Street trees shall be provided along each such public street frontage in 
accordance with §604 of this Chapter, at a ratio of not less than one (1) shade tree for each 
forty-five (45) feet of street frontage which demonstrates the open spac e to accommodate 
the tree, planted within twenty (20) feet of the curbed cartway in non -grated tree wells or 
within a planting strip located between the curb and the sidewalk. Each street tree shall be 
a minimum of two and one-half (2.5) inch caliper measured at six (6) inches above grade, 
shall be limbed up to six (6) feet in height, and shall be of a species selected from the 
recommended tree lists in §604 or otherwise approved by the Borough. Street trees shall 
be planted in an evenly spaced, straight row unless a staggered or naturalistic alignment is 
approved by the Shade Tree Commission. Required street trees shall be shown on the 
DRAFT

landscape plan required by §507A.7, and the applicant shall make payment to the Borough 
for each required street tree in accordance with the Borough fee schedule prior to the 
release of plans for recording, whereupon the Borough shall purchase and install the trees 
as provided in §604. No existing tree within a public right -of-way shall be removed or 
disturbed without the approval of the Shade Tree Commission. Street trees provided under 
this subsection may be counted toward the perimeter landscaping requirement of §507A.5, 
but shall not be counted toward the parking area shade tree requirement of §507A.4. 
C. Pedestrian Benches and Seating Areas. Pedestrian benches shall be installed along each 
such public street frontage at intervals of not more than three hundred (300) feet, with each 
bench centered between a pair of street trees, and with at least one ben ch located at each 
transit stop along the frontage. Each bench shall match, in design, material, and color, the 
standard public bench installed along the north side of the Penn Avenue and Park Road 
intersection, or a Borough -approved equivalent. In additio n, along each public street 
frontage exceeding one thousand (1,000) feet in length, at least one (1) landscaped 
pedestrian seating area shall be provided at a location shown on the Master Redevelopment 
Plan. Each seating area shall contain not less than four hundred (400) square feet, shall be 
directly accessible from the public sidewalk, and shall include not fewer than two (2) 
benches, shade trees or a shade structure, ornamental planting, and pedestrian -scale 
lighting. Seating areas provided under this s ubsection shall count toward the Open Area 
requirement of §504A. 
D. Coordination with PennDOT. Streetscape improvements within or affecting the right -
of-way of State Hill Road (SR 3055), Route 422 (SR 0422), or any other state highway 
shall be coordinated with and subject to the approval of the Pennsylvania Department o f 
Transportation in accordance with subsection 5 of this §512A. 
E. Plan; Construction; Maintenance. All streetscape improvements shall be shown on the 
Master Redevelopment Plan, shall be constructed prior to or concurrently with the phase 
of development they serve, and shall be perpetually operated, maintained, repaire d, and 
replaced by the Lot Owners Association pursuant to §511A, except to the extent any such 
improvement is accepted for dedication and maintenance by the Borough or the 
Pennsylvania Department of Transportation. Street trees purchased and installed by the 
Borough pursuant to subsection B shall be maintained by the Borough, and the 
maintenance obl igation of the Lot Owners Association under this subsection shall not 
extend to those trees. 
§513A. Master Redevelopment Plan; Procedure. 
1. An applicant seeking to develop under this Part shall submit a Master Redevelopment Plan 
depicting, for the overall redevelopment site: existing and proposed lots and lot lines; existing and 
proposed buildings, uses, and gross floor areas; parking areas , parking computations, and any 
shared parking plan prepared under §506A.3 and Parking Demand Analysis; the bicycle and 
pedestrian improvements and ride -share loading zones for which credits are claimed under 
§506A.5, together with the depiction of the red evelopment site in relation to the Official Bike 
DRAFT

Route Map required by §506A.5.E; landscaping, parking area greenspace, and shade tree plans 
under §507A, including the landscape plan required by §507A.7; open space under §508A; shared 
stormwater management facilities under §509A; the composite utility plan under §510A, including 
the public and private demarcation required by §510A.3; the streetscape improvements required 
under §512A.6; the traffic impact study required under §512A.4; a phasing schedule under 
§501A.5.G; and the proposed Declaration and Association documents under §511A. 
2. The Master Redevelopment Plan shall be reviewed by the Borough Planning Commission and 
approved by Borough Council in conjunction with land development approval under the Borough 
Subdivision and Land Development Ordinance. Minor modifications consistent with the approved 
Master Redevelopment Plan, as determined by the Zoning Officer in consultation with the 
Borough Engineer, shall not require reapproval; other modifications shall be submitted as revisions 
to the Master Redevelopment Plan.
