DEPARTMENT OF ANIMAL PROTECTION 
 
2027 FULL-SERVICE CONTRACT 
AT $3.32 PER CAPITA 
 
1 YEAR AGREEMENT 
between 
The Animal Rescue League of Berks County 
and 
Municipality of Wyomissing Borough 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
DRAFT

 
 
 
2 
 
 
This agreement (the "Agreement") is made this _______day of  ___________, 2026, by 
and between the Animal Rescue League of Berks County, 58 Kennel Road, Birdsboro, PA 
19508, a Pennsylvania non- profit corporation (the "ARL "), and Wyomissing Borough, the 
"Municipality"). 
WHEREAS, the Municipality is statutorily charged with the obligation to care for and 
control stray dogs within the Municipality’s boundaries; 
WHEREAS, the ARL has the means to provide that care and control  for stray dogs 
and stray cats , including the requisite knowledge, expertise, personnel, equipment, and 
legal capacity; and 
WHEREAS, in order to humanely, efficiently, and cost -effectively discharge its legal 
duties to care for and control stray animals  within its boundaries, the Municipality  wishes 
to engage the services of the ARL which includes care and control for both stray dogs and 
stray cats. 
NOW, THEREFORE, in consideration of the mutual covenants herein contained and 
intending to be legally bound, the parties hereto agree that: 
A. Term of the Agreement 
 
The ARL shall commence providing the services specified in this Agreement 
on the 1 st day of January 2027 at 12:0 0am and shall cease providing said 
services on December 31, 2027 at 11:59pm (the “Term”). 
 
B. The Services 
 
1. Statutes to be enforced: The ARL shall take all reasonable actions necessary 
to enforce within the Municipality’s boundaries the following “Animal Control” 
laws: 
a. the Pennsylvania Dog Law, Act of De c. 7, 1982, P.L. 784, No. 225, 
Art. 1, s. 101. et. seq., 3 P.S. § 459-101, et.-seq. (the “Dog Law”); 
b. the Rabies Prevention and Control in Domestic Animals and 
Wildlife Act, Dec. 15, 1986, P.L. 1610, No. 181 §1 et.-seq., 3 P.S. §455.1 
et.-seq.; 
c. such other statutes or regulations which may impose certain 
duties with respect to any municipal animal control ordinances, 
which statutes and regulations the reasonable enforcement of 
which the Municipality shall delegate in writing to the ARL, and 
which upon the latter’s written acceptable shall also become 
part of this Agreement.  
 
 
DRAFT

 
 
 
3 
 
2. Assignment of APO Officers:     The ARL shall assign Department of Animal 
Protection Officers (the “APO Officer”) to respond and provide services to the 
Municipality. It will be at the sole discretion of the ARL how shifts and 
personnel are assigned. The Municipality acknowledges and that the APO will 
be responsible for providing services to other municipalities during that shift. 
The Municipality also understands, acknowledges and agrees that while the 
ARL will make every effort to respond expeditiously, there is no guarantee as 
to the specificity of time of such response. 
 
3. Acceptance of Stray Animals:      The ARL agrees to accept and care for stray 
dogs and cats (the “Stray Animals”) showing signs of ownership  originating 
from within the Municipality’s municipal borders. Signs of ownership include, 
but are not limited to; Microchips, collars, tags, tattoos, clothing articles, 
surgical scarring and information acquired through finder/witness 
statements and shall be determined in the ARL’s sole discretion. In the case 
of citizens of the Municipality who truly cannot transport stray animals to ARL 
due to dangerous or severely injured animals , lack of a vehicle or license,  or 
are elderly/infirm, the ARL shall pick up and transport the Stray Animals to the 
ARL facility during the ARL’s Normal Operati ng Hours, which shall be set  
exclusively by the ARL, and which may be revised from time to time in the 
ARL’s sole discretion. The Municipality shall assure that such animals will be 
reasonably confined or restrained in a humane and effective manner prior to 
the ARL’s response to transport said animal(s). Stray Animals accepted will 
also include those brought to the ARL  facility by ARL  A PO, by the 
Municipality’s designated representative(s), its  P olice Officers, State Police 
Officers, County D og Wardens, and by its private citizens. The ARL shall 
determine a disposition for each stray animal at the sole discretion of the ARL, 
including, but not limited to: a doption, transferring to another rescue  
organization, humane euthanasia or returning to the field. Any and all 
dispositions shall be determined based on current Pennsylvania Laws and 
internal operational procedures. 
 
4. Investigation of Complaints:      The ARL shall investigate all complaints of 
violations of Dog Law as listed under Agreement section B.1 (a- c) received 
from the Municipality , its Police Officers  or its residents, seven (7) days per 
week. The method of this investigation shall be at the sole discretion of the 
ARL and the APO. 
 
 
5. Resident Calls:     The ARL and the APO will respond to calls for service from 
residents originating within the borders of the Municipality. The Municipality 
will make all appropriate efforts to advise its residents to contact the ARL  
directly to ensure the proper handling of such calls and to provide continuity 
DRAFT

 
 
 
4 
 
of service to the Municipality. Contact information for the ARL will be listed on 
the Municipality’s website as the point of contact for animal control services. 
Calls for service can also be sent through County dispatch channels. 
 
6. Issuance of Citations and Warrants:     The ARL and the APO will work diligently 
to be proactive in response to the enforcement of State and local laws. The 
ARL and the APO will, at their sole discretion, issue non -traffic citations and 
Criminal Complaints for violations of State and local animal control laws, 
including dog laws and animal control ordinances. To the extent permitted by 
law, all fines and penalties collected as the result of any service provided by 
the ARL under this Agreement shall be paid to the court. The only exception 
to the foregoing shall be if the state, county, or local law enforcement 
authorities request that the ARL shall hold an animal for the duration of a 
criminal prosecution involving such animals, in which case the ARL shall be 
entitled to apply only such fines or penalties to the cost of maintenance or 
care of such animal(s) by the ARL. 
 
7. Abandoned Animals:     The ARL shall respond to all requests from Local Police, 
State Police, State Constables, and Sheriff’s Deputies of the Municipality, and 
shall take custody of any animal(s) left inside a residence upon eviction, special 
circumstances, or other absence of its residents.  
 
8. Municipality Assistance: The ARL and the APO shall provide any assistance to 
the Municipality and it s Local and State Police, State Constables, Sheriff’s 
Deputies and any other Municipal worker with the control and confinement 
of animals in matters including, but not limited to, search warrants, welfare 
checks, home inspections and arrests. The ARL shall detain and house any 
animal(s) originating from these circumstances. 
Additionally, the ARL shall provide housing for any and all animals the 
Municipality and its local and State Police, State Constables and Sheriff’s 
Deputies seize in the pursuit of criminal investigation. The Municipality or its 
local and State Police, State Constables or Sheriff’s Deputies shall designate a 
coordinator to communicate to and receive communications from the ARL 
concerning any and all logistics pertaining to the seized animal(s) and shall 
seek Cost of Care on behalf of the ARL. Restitutions shall be submitted to the 
ARL once Cost of Care is granted. The ARL shall provide all records of care, 
and maintain security, both physically and digitally, of the animal(s). 
9. Community Outreach:     In an effort to proactively address the systematic 
problem of animal neglect and homelessness, the ARL and the Municipality 
will, from time to time and at their mutual convenience, through their normal 
communication channels with the public, work together to: (a) educate the 
public about the value and advantages of spaying and neutering; (b) provide 
DRAFT

 
 
 
5 
 
information on educational outre ach programs that seek to enhance 
relationships between pet owners and their pets and reduce the numbers of 
animals in distress; (c) educate the public on how to responsibly care for a pet 
and how to develop a strong owner–animal bond through proper training and 
behavior modification techniques; (d) raise public awareness of the ARL’s  
services and goals; and (e) participate in community meetings and other 
public events. 
 
 
C. Fees & Billing 
 
1. The Municipality shall pay the ARL an Annual Fee of $3.32 per resident of the 
Municipality for each contract year. The determination of “per resident” shall 
be based on the most recent 2020 Census population numbers. The 
Municipality reserves the right to satisfy the balance of this contract in one (1) 
annual fee, two (2) biannual payments or four (4) quarterly payments. The 
agreed payment schedule shall be made, in writing, by the Municipality upon 
return of the signed agreement. 
 
D. Other Provisions 
 
1. ARL Employees:     The ARL is an independent contractor to the Municipality 
and none of its agents, officers, or employees shall be construed as, or 
represent themselves as, employees of the Municipality. The ARL  shall be 
responsible for workers compensation insurance for its employees, including 
the AP O Officers, as well as insurance for its vehicles and equipment. A 
certificate of insurance coverage for the foregoing shall be provided by the 
ARL to the Municipality within thirty (30) days of acceptance and execution of 
this Agreement and payment in full. 
 
2. General Liability Insurance:     The Municipality shall assure that the ARL , its 
Officers and directors, and its employees are covered by the Municipality’s 
insurance for liability purposes. For the purpose of this Agreement, the 
Municipality shall obtain and maintain in full force, at no charge to the ARL , 
the following insurance coverages in not less than the following amounts and 
terms: 
 
a. Comprehensive General Liability Insurance on a broad form 
occurrence basis with a limit of not less than $100,000 per 
occurrence, and for not less than the Life of this Agreement; 
 
b. Each of the policy/ies of insurance shall name ARL as a named 
additional insured; shall require the issuer or insurer to provide 
ARL with at least thirty (30) days prior written notice of any 
DRAFT

 
 
 
6 
 
material change or cancellation of the policy/ies; and shall be 
primary to any insurance coverages separately maintained by 
the ARL; 
 
c. Not later than the execution of this Agreement, the Municipality 
shall deliver to the ARL a copy of the Declaration Page and of the 
Policy, evidencing the coverage of the ARL  under each policy 
described above and according to the above terms. 
 
3. Removal of Dead Animals:     The ARL shall have no responsibility or obligation 
to remove dead animals, domestic or wild, from roadways or private property. 
The Municipality may, at their own discretion, advise the ARL of any and all  
appropriate agencies who provide such service, and the ARL will, upon 
request, release this information to any party seeking such service. 
 
4. Payment upon termination by the Municipality :  The Municipality 
understands and acknowledges that in order to voluntarily terminate this 
Agreement before its normally-scheduled ending date, the Municipality must 
pay the most current Per Capita total reflective of the remaining Term left on 
the agreement at the most current annual rate. 
 
5. Force Majeure:        The ARL reserves the right to reduce, limit or suspend any 
service listed within in the event of weather, facility, animal population, staff 
population, or local catastrophes (“Force Majeure Event”). In the event the ARL 
determines a reduction, limitation or suspension of services is warranted, the 
ARL shall: 
 
a.              Notify the Municipality of the Force Majeure Event by registered 
or certified mail, postage prepaid, return receipt requested, or by 
guaranteed overnight delivery service, in addition to email , in a timely 
fashion. 
b. Declare a time frame in which the ARL reasonably believes the 
reduction, limitation or suspension of services may occur.  
c.              Provide frequent updates, by the aforementioned  postal 
delivery options and email advising the Municipality on the current 
status of the Force Majeure Event , including any increase or decrease 
of time of reduction, limitation or suspension of services. 
d. Assist the Municipality in advising the citizens of the Municipality 
of the reduction, limitation, or suspension of services. 
       
 
 
 
 
DRAFT

 
 
 
7

be sent by registered mail, certified mail, postage prepaid, return receipt 
requested, or by guaranteed overnight delivery service. Notices shall be 
addressed as follows: 
 
If to the ARL: 
 
Chief Executive Officer

7. Non-Assignability:     This Agreement may not be assigned or transferred by  
either Party without the written consent of the other. All assignments of rights 
are prohibited under this subsection, whether they are voluntary or 
involuntary, by merger (of any kind), consolidation, dissolution, operation of 
law, or any other manner. N otwithstanding the foregoing, either Party may 
assign its rights under this Agreement without the prior written consent of 
the other in connection with a sale of its business as a whole or substantially 
all of the assets of its business. Any purported assignment of rights in violation 
of this Section is void. 
 
8. Entire Agreement:     This Agreement contains the entire Agreement of the 
Parties with respect to its subject matter and supersedes all existing and all 
other oral, written, or other communications between the Parties concerning 
this subject matter.

together shall constitute one and the same instrument.

subsequent writing signed by both Parties.

2026 Invasive Plant Control 
Forest Mowing Letter of Agreement 
 
I. This letter serves as an agreement between Shultz Forest Management and Consulting LLC 
(contractor) and the Borough of Wyomissing (owner). 
II. As the contractor for the forest mulching project located at 2120 Parkview Drive, 
Wyomissing, PA 19610, Shultz Forest Management and Consulting LLC agrees to: 
 
a. Utilize forestry mower to cut and mulch invasive and/or undesirable brush on 16.2 
acres of forest in Stand 1 as outlined in the 2026 Forest Management Plan. 
1. Primary target species is non-native bush honeysuckle. 
b. Notify the Borough of Wyomissing  
1. One month prior to estimated start date 
2. One week prior to definite start date 
 
 
III. This contract does not cover: 
 
 a.   Mulching or chipping of any stems over four inches in diameter 
  
 b.   Removal of any plant material from the site 
 
a. Raking, moving, or piling any wood chips  
 
b. Moving or piling any brush, logs, or wood chips 
 
IV.  The total cost of the project will be $25,300.00, including all time, equipment, and materials 
dedicated to the project. The treatment area was derived from maps and descriptions provided in 
the 2026 Forest Management Plan.   
V.  The owner, the Borough of Wyomissing, agrees that the project acreage and details are 
correct and will not contest upon completion of the job. 
VI.  Any changes to the scope of work such as adding tasks or increasing treatment area will 
require a change order. Additional charges for time, equipment, and/or materials to complete 
additional tasks will be applied to the final invoice and/or may require an additional deposit. 
 
DRAFT

 
VII.  The owner, the Borough of Wyomissing, agrees to: 
a. Pay Shultz Forest management and Consulting LLC the cost specified above after the 
completion of the treatment in accordance with the following terms. 
1. Pay Shultz Forest Management and Consulting LLC by the invoice due date 
which will be 10 days from the completion of the treatment.  
2. Pay Shultz Forest Management and Consulting LLC a finance charge of 5.0% per 
month for any outstanding balance past the invoice due date. 
b. Grant Shultz Forest Management and Consulting LLC staff and subcontractors access 
to the project site during the treatment phase and any necessary follow-up inspection. 
c. Submit this signed agreement with a 10% deposit ($2,530.00), which signifies that 
the owners are in agreement with the terms provided in this letter and that the 
contractor has permission to begin the project as they see fit. 
d. Acknowledge that the use of equipment such as forest mowers, chainsaws, and/or 
brush cutters is inherently dangerous, will not interfere with contractor while 
working, and will not enter the project site without prior communication with the 
operator, an escort, and proper safety equipment. 
e. Notify the public of the project and close all trails within 300 feet of the treatment 
area during the project. 
 
VIII.  Landowner and Contractor Protection 
 
Nature of Work: 
 
The owner, the Borough of Wyomissing, understands that the forest mowing alone will not 
effectively control most invasive species present. The landowner understands that Shultz Forest 
Management and Consulting LLC cannot control environmental factors (i.e. weather) and plant 
behavior and, unless grossly negligent in performing said work, is not responsible for 
undesirable effects of forest mowing. Undesirable effects may include, but are not limited to, 
damage or death to species attempting to be released or lack of effectiveness to targeted species. 
 
Insurance: 
Shultz Forest Management and Consulting LLC also agrees to maintain valid General Liability 
coverage of $1,000,000 per occurrence and Worker’s Compensation coverage in accordance 
with state minimums. 
Waiver of Subrogation: 
To the fullest extent permitted by law, the Borough of Wyomissing, shall indemnify and hold 
harmless Shultz Forest Management and Consulting LLC, its owner, employees, subcontractors,

volunteers, and others working on behalf of Shultz Forest Management and Consulting LLC 
from any and all liability or responsibility to the Borough of Wyomissing or anyone claiming

suits, actions, causes of action, liabilities, fines, penalties, costs, expenses, damages, and losses , 
including but not limited to attorneys’ fees and costs, arising out of or resulting from the work 
performed by Shultz Forest Management and Consulting LLC, its owner, employees, sub-

Policy Briefing Summary  
Purchase of Automated External Defibrillators (AEDs) 
Background and Justification 
The Wyomissing Fire Department is requesting authorization to purchase ten (10) recertified 
LIFEPAK 1000 Automated External Defibrillators (AEDs) and associated equipment at a total 
cost of $14,152. 
AEDs are critical lifesaving equipment used during sudden cardiac arrest. Having reliable and 
readily accessible AEDs allows emergency responders to deliver potentially lifesaving treatment 
in the critical minutes before more advanced medical care is available. Maintaining this 
equipment is a fundamental component of the Borough's emergency response capabilities. 
The proposed purchase includes ten recertified AEDs at $1,199 each, five-year warranties for 
each unit, replacement batteries, and adult and pediatric electrode pads. Purchasing recertified 
equipment provides the Borough with an opportunity to acquire essential lifesaving technology 
at a lower cost than purchasing comparable new equipment, while providing additional 
protection through the included warranties. 
Financial Impact 
The total proposed expenditure is $14,152, based on the November 2, 2026 estimate provided 
by Equipment Express LLC of Bethlehem, Pennsylvania. The estimate is valid through December 
3, 2026. 
Recommendation 
It is recommended that Borough Council authorize the purchase of ten recertified LIFEPAK 1000 
AEDs and associated equipment from Equipment Express LLC in an amount not to exceed 
$14,152. This investment supports the Borough's public safety mission and ensures that 
emergency responders have access to essential lifesaving equipment.

1 
Wyomissing Public Library 
Board Meeting 
Monday, October 19, 2026 
 
The meeting was called to Order at 7:01 p.m. by David Walker 
 
Board Members Present: Tracy Beaky, Andrea Bensusan, Kristie Brunner, Jim Christie, David Fake, Rose 
Kennedy, Danielle Nuhfer, Charlie Perkins, Gretchen Platt, Jennafer Reilly, Heidi Reuter, Michelle Van 
Buren, David Walker 
 
Borough Council Representative: None 
 
Library Director: Colleen Stamm 
 
A MOTION was made by Rose Kennedy to approve the September 2026 Board Meeting minutes, and it 
was seconded by Jim Christie. The motion was carried unanimously. 
 
President’s Report: David Walker 
1. 2027 Budget 
a. David said that we need to vote on the budget tonight.  He asked if there were any 
comments or questions.  He said that we are asking for an increase from the Borough of 
Wyomissing over last year even though the borough may not give us an increase. 
b. David brought up the technology reserve fund we discussed in April.  There is about 
$40,000 of technology that will need to be replaced about every five years; we 
discussed in April building up a reserve fund for that expense over 5 years, $8,000 a 
year.  David wants to add a line item separate from Infrastructure Improvements to 
account for that and call it the Technology Reserve Fund.  Michelle suggested budgeting 
for a smaller number this year and increasing that number over the next 5 years if 
needed to reach our goal.  Colleen asked about where we are finding the money to fund 
it and brought up that Steve Pottiger said we could take out 3% of the endowment to 
cover that cost.  Kristie asked where the 3% is coming from.  David explained that the 
money is coming from the Schwab “endowment” and that 3% is about $18,000.  Charlie 
clarified that this is money that may be spent but does not have to be spent each fiscal 
year.  David said the endowment and the reserves hopefully make us less vulnerable to 
outside funds but we need to be protected.  David asked for a motion for $5,000 for the 
Technology Reserve Fund.  
i. A MOTION was made by Charlie Perkins to approve $5,000 for the Technology 
Reserve Fund and it was seconded by Heidi Reuter.   The motion was carried 
unanimously. 
David asked for a motion to accept the 2027 budget.  
ii. A MOTION was made by Charlie Perkins to approve the 2027 budget and it was 
seconded by Michelle VanBuren.   The motion was carried unanimously. 
c. David said we will revisit the budget after we find out how much we are getting from 
outside entities in the new year. 
2. David reported that the library will be the first item on the agenda for the West Reading 
Borough Council meeting on October 20th. Heidi, David, and Colleen will attend the meeting 
and request funding for the library.  Jim asked if what we are asking for is based on West 
DRAFT

2 
Reading residents' usage.  David confirmed that this is how the funding amount was determined 
and noted that a similar funding request will be presented as it was last year. About 12% of the 
library’s circulation is West Reading residents.  Heidi Reuter said we should share our total 
budget, West Reading resident usage, and program offerings. 
3. Executive Committee
a. We need a new Nominating committee chair. We have four board members leaving at
the end of the year, and we will need to recruit two people, one board member and one
alternate.  David asked if anyone was interested.  The Board discussed the Nominating
Committee Chair position, but no one volunteered for this position. Colleen will begin
advertising for board vacancies.
Treasurer’s Report: Jim Christie (Steve Ohnsman was absent) 
1. Vanguard funds have been transferred to Conner’s Investment but has not shown up on this
month’s report.  There was a statement sent on October 7th that the funds have been
transferred.  Our fundraising year-to-date is $37,331.  Donations are at $32,700 at the end of
September and the budgeted expectation is $40,000.  We had higher than expected expenses
this month due to higher cost for copier maintenance and IT.  Library materials are double that
of the usual monthly expense, but we are behind our spending for the year.  A check in the
amount of $6,375 for our financial review was sent on October 1st.  Jim reported that Steve
Ohnsman is continuing to talk with Senator Schwank’s office about slightly reducing our state
funding, so we are not subjected to a yearly audit.  David asked when we find out how much the
state is going to have us. Colleen said that comes at the beginning of the year.
Librarian’s Report: Colleen Stamm 
1. Library Usage Statistics for September 2026
a. Materials circulation: 3,468 items. That was a 5% decrease compared to the same
month last year.
b. Overdrive eBooks: 1,410 downloads; that was an 18% increase compared to the same
month last year.
c. People: 3,367 people entered the library; that was an 18% increase compared to the
same month last year.
d. Computer Users: there were 286 accesses for the month; that was a 5% increase
compared to the same month last year.
e. Children’s programming: 665 patrons participated in the children’s activities; that was a
20% increase compared to the same month last year.
f. Adult Programming: 249 patrons took part in our activities; a 33% decrease compared to
the same month last year.
g. AWE Early Literacy Stations: 49 accesses; a 70% decrease compared to the same month
last year.
h. Wireless Internet Users: 225 people used the library Wi-Fi access; a 5% increase
compared to the same month last year.
i. Hoopla: there were 423 accesses for the month; a 64% increase compared to the same
month last year.
2. Other
a. Colleen was in Harrisburg with the Pennsylvania Library Association to talk with elected
officials about the library.  She talked with Rep Johanny Cepeda-Freytiz and stopped by
Senator Judy Schwank’s office.  They are both very supportive of the library.
DRAFT

3 
b. Daniella and Colleen went to Spooky Hollow and also attended the WASD Back to School 
night. 
c. The Pumpkin People of Wyomissing display is out; Daniella worked on it with the 
preschool storytime kids.  
d. The Berks County Commissioners were at the library for their Town Hall and Colleen said 
it was the second largest town hall they have held this year. 
 
Committee Reports: 
1. Fundraising/Community Affairs: Tracy Beaky 
a. Holiday baskets- Tracy reported that board members have been dropping off 
items.  Gretchen said she will put an ask out for what we need after she goes through 
the donations.  David Fake shared what gift cards he was able to gather from local 
businesses.  Michelle shared that she donated money to buy items to fill out the 
baskets.  Gretchen suggested making the Election Day bake sale a “give what you want” 
donation for the baked goods instead of setting a price for them.  Tracy asked if the 
price of raffle tickets should be increased. Several board members expressed concern 
that a price increase might reduce ticket sales. The Board agreed to keep raffle ticket 
prices the same. 
b. Trolley Tour - David inquired about the status of the event and asked if we have secured 
someone to be Santa. Colleen reported that she has several potential candidates, and 
Danielle Nuhfer noted that her friend who served as Santa last year is willing to return 
this year. Colleen shared she did book the trolley, and the event will be held on Friday, 
December 11th.  Michelle asked if the library wanted her to sponsor the trolley again, 
and Colleen said yes.  Michelle has sponsored the trolley for several years and the board 
offered their thanks for the support. 
 
Old Business 
1. None 
 
New Business 
1. None 
 
Meeting adjourned at 7.49 p.m. 
 
Meeting Minutes submitted by Jennafer Reilly 
 
Next Board Meeting: Monday, November 16, 2026 at 7:00 p.m.

• Project is substantially complete.

• Advising Dept. of Public Works and Fire Dept regarding in house renovations to workout room and in lower
level of fire house.

Western Berks Ambulance Association 1 / 4
WESTERN BERKS AMBULANCE ASSOCIATION
Municipal Performance Report
Wyomissing Borough
September 2026 | Year to date through September 30, 2026
SEPTEMBER CALLS 2026 YTD CALLS MONTHLY AVERAGE
254 2,249 249.9
Executive Summary
Western Berks Ambulance Association recorded 254 calls in Wyomissing Borough during September 2026,
bringing January-September activity to 2,249 calls. Volume decreased 11.2% from August (286 calls).
September was 1.6% above the nine-month average of 249.9 calls.
Monthly volume is explicitly dated in the source. Supporting widgets are reported as displayed, with their own
counts and limitations; matching totals alone do not verify their reporting periods.
